be any lifting for Lebesgue measure satisfying
\eta_{0}([0,1/2))=[0,1/2)
, and for measurable sets
E\subseteq[0,1]
, let
\eta(E)=\eta_{0}(E)
unless
E
has the form
E=[0,1/2)\setminus\{u(x)\}
, in which case set
\eta([0,1/2)\setminus\{u(x)\})=[0,1/2)\cup\{h(x)\}
.

Let
E\subseteq X\times Y
be
\upsilon
-measurable. By
[
3
]
, Proposition 5.2 (iii), we have
E=_{\upsilon}L:=X\times A
for some measurable set
A
. The set
E\,\triangle\,(X\times A)
is contained in
(P\times Y)\cup(X\times Q)
for some measure zero sets
P,Q
, so
E\cap(P^{c}\times Q^{c})=P^{c}\times(A\cap Q^{c})
. If
A=_{\nu}[0,1/2)
fails, then we also do not have
E_{x}=_{\nu}[0,1/2)
for any
x
and hence
\eta_{\bullet}(E)\cap(P^{c}\times Y)=P^{c}\times\eta(A)
. This gives for any
y\in\eta(A)
,
[\eta_{\bullet}(E)]^{y}\cap P^{c}=P^{c}
and therefore
[\eta_{\bullet}(E)]^{y}
has measure one and
[\varphi(E)]^{y}=\delta([\eta_{\bullet}(E)]^{y})=X
.
When
y\notin\eta(A)
, we get
[\eta_{\bullet}(E)]^{y}\cap P^{c}=\emptyset
and therefore
[\eta_{\bullet}(E)]^{y}
has measure zero and
[\varphi(E)]^{y}=\delta([\eta_{\bullet}(E)]^{y})=\emptyset
. It follows that
\varphi(E)=X\times\eta(A)
and hence
\varphi(E)=_{\upsilon}E
.

If
A=_{\nu}[0,1/2)
holds, then for some
N\in\varSigma_{0}
, we have for
x\in N^{c}
that
E_{x}\in T
and
E_{x}=_{\nu}A
, so
[\eta_{\bullet}(E)]_{x}=\eta(E_{x})=[0,1/2)
or
[0,1/2)\cup\{w(x)\}
for some
w(x)\in[1/2,1]
. Only countable many fibers
w^{-1}(y)
of the (partially defined) function
w
can be measurable of positive measure. For any other value of
y
,
[\eta_{\bullet}(E)]^{y}
is of measure zero or not measurable, so
[\varphi(E)]^{y}=\emptyset
. It follows that
\varphi(E)\cap(N^{c}\times Y)
equals the union of
N^{c}\times[0,1/2)
and countably many sets of measure zero of the form
\delta(w^{-1}(y))\times\{y\}
and hence equals
E
modulo
\upsilon
.

However, identifying
u
with its graph
\{(x,u(x)):x\in X\}
, for the set
E=(X\times[0,1/2))\setminus u
, all the sections
[\eta_{\bullet}(E)]^{y}=h^{-1}(y)
are nonmeasurable for
y\in[1/2,1]
, so
\eta
is not a weak
2
-marginal for
\Upsilon
.

Proposition 8.19
.

Assume that
\mu
is complete and that
[C]
and
[\overline{C}]
hold.
Let
\delta\in P^{p}(\mu)
,
\eta\in P^{p}(\nu)
. Assume that
(\delta,\eta)
is
\mathcal{L}^{p}(\upsilon)
-preserving.
If
\eta
is a weak
2
-marginal with respect to
\mathcal{L}^{p}(\upsilon)
, then

\delta\odot\eta\in P^{p}(\upsilon)
.

Proof.

The product
\delta\odot\eta
satisfies condition

(l2)
by Lemma
8.5
(iii), and
(\delta\odot\eta)(f)=f
for
f
constant, by Lemma
8.5
(iv), so we need only to show condition
(l1)
, which follows by Proposition
8.11
.
∎

Proposition 8.20
.

Assume that
\mu
is complete and that
[C]
and
[\overline{C}]
hold.
Let
\delta\in{V}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\infty}(\mu)
,
\eta\in{V}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\infty}(\nu)
. If
(\delta,\eta)
is
\mathcal{L}^{\infty}(\upsilon)
-preserving and
\eta
is a weak
2
-marginal with respect to
\mathcal{L}^{\infty}(\upsilon)
and generates
\mathcal{L}^{\infty}(\mu)
-sections, then
\delta\odot\eta\in{V}^{\infty}_{\mathbb{R}}(\upsilon)
.

Proof.

By Proposition
8.19
we get
\delta\odot\eta\in{P}^{\infty}(\upsilon)
and by Lemma
8.5
(vi) the product
\delta\odot\eta
is a linear map.
∎

Corollary 8.21
.

Assume that
\mu
is complete and that
[C]
and
[\overline{C}]
hold.
If
(\delta,\eta)\in\mathcal{G}(\mu)\times\mathcal{G}(\nu)

is
\mathcal{L}^{\infty}(\upsilon)
-preserving and
\eta
is a weak
2
-marginal with respect to
\mathcal{L}^{\infty}(\upsilon)
and generates
\mathcal{L}^{\infty}(\mu)
-sections, then
\delta\odot\eta\in\mathcal{G}(\upsilon)
.

Proof.

By Proposition
8.20
we get
\delta\odot\eta\in{V}^{\infty}_{\mathbb{R}}(\upsilon)
; hence
\delta\odot\eta\in\mathcal{G}(\upsilon)
by Lemma
8.5
(viii).
∎

Question 8.22
.

Let
(\delta,\eta)\in{V}^{\infty}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mu)\times{V}^{\infty}_{\mathbb{R}}(\nu)
preserve measurability, and let
\eta
be a weak 2-marginal with respect to
\mathcal{L}^{\infty}(\mu\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\nu)
. Assume that
\delta\odot\eta\in{V}^{\infty}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mu\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\nu)
. Does
\eta
generate
\mu
-measurable sections in
\mathcal{L}^{\infty}(\mu)
?
What if we assume also that
(\delta,\eta)\in\mathcal{G}(\mu)\times\mathcal{G}(\nu)
and
\delta\odot\eta\in\mathcal{G}(\mu\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\nu)
?

The next result is complementary to Theorem 2.6 from
[
26
]
.

Theorem 8.23
.

Assume that
\mu
and
\nu
are complete.
Let
\delta\in\mathcal{G}(\mu)
,
\eta\in{A}\mathcal{G}(\nu)
. If
(\delta,\eta)
is
\mathcal{L}^{\infty}(\upsilon)
-preserving and
\eta
generates
\mathcal{L}^{\infty}(\mu)
-sections, then
\pi:=\delta\odot\eta\in\mathcal{G}(\mu\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\nu)
and the following hold.

(i)

\pi\in\delta\otimes\eta
.

(ii)

[\pi(f)]^{y}=\delta([\pi(f)]^{y})\;\;\mbox{for all}\;\;f\in\mathcal{L}^{\infty}(\mu\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\nu)\;\;\mbox{and all}\;\;y\in{Y}
.

Proof.

Since
\eta\in{A}\mathcal{G}(\nu)
and
\mu
and
\nu
are complete, it follows by
[
26
]
, Proposition 2.4
that there exists
\varphi\in\mathcal{G}(\mu\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\nu)
such that for every
f\in\mathcal{L}^{\infty}(\mu\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\nu)
there exists a set
N_{f}\in\varSigma_{0}
such that

[\varphi(f)]_{x}=\eta([\varphi(f)]_{x})
for every
x\in X\setminus N_{f}
.
By Lemma
6.18
, taking
\upsilon=\mu\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\nu
and
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{L}^{\infty}(\upsilon)
, we get that
\eta_{\bullet}
satisfies
(l1)
and hence
\eta
is a weak
2
-marginal with respect to
\mathcal{L}^{\infty}(\mu\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\nu)
. It then follows by Corollary
8.21
that
\pi:=\delta\odot\eta\in\mathcal{G}(\mu\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\nu)
, and so applying Lemma
8.5
, (ii), (v), we obtain the validity of the statements (i) and (ii).
∎

The next result corresponds to
[
27
]
, Proposition 5.1, concerning liftings for sets, and has a similar proof, which we enclose for completeness.

Proposition 8.24
.

Assume that
\mu
is complete.
If
(\delta,\eta)\in\Lambda^{\infty}(\mu)\times\Lambda^{\infty}(\nu)
is
\mathcal{L}^{\infty}(\upsilon)
-preserving and
\eta
is
2
-marginal with respect to
\mathcal{L}^{\infty}(\mu\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\nu)
the the following statements are equivalent

(i)

\eta
is
\mu
-smooth;

(ii)

\delta\odot\eta\in\Lambda^{\infty}(\mu\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\nu)
.

Proof.

For (i)
\Rightarrow
(ii), by Corollary
8.21
we get
\delta\odot\eta\in\mathcal{G}(\mu\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\nu)
, implying along with Lemma
8.5
(vii) that
\delta\odot\eta\in\Lambda^{\infty}(\mu\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\nu)
.

For (ii)
\Rightarrow
(i),
let
f\in\mathcal{L}^{\infty}(\mu\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\nu)
and let
g=1-f
. By Proposition
2.19
(ii), there is an
N\in\varSigma_{0}
such that for
x\notin N
.
f_{x},g_{x}\in\mathcal{L}^{\infty}(\nu)
. When
x\notin N
, we have
f_{x}+g_{x}=1
, so
\eta(f_{x})+\eta(g_{x})=\eta(1)=1
. Thus, we have for any
y\in Y
the equation
[\eta_{\bullet}(f)]^{y}(x)+[\eta_{\bullet}(g)]^{y}(x)=1
for
x\notin N
. Since
\mu
is complete, it follows that
[\eta_{\bullet}(f)]^{y}\in\mathcal{L}^{\infty}(\mu)
if and only if
[\eta_{\bullet}(g)]^{y}\in\mathcal{L}^{\infty}(\mu)
.
Since for any
y\in Y
we have

1=[(\delta\odot\eta)(1)]^{y}=[(\delta\odot\eta)(f+g)]^{y}=[(\delta\odot\eta)(f)]^{y}+[(\delta\odot\eta)(g)]^{y},

the conditions
[\eta_{\bullet}(f)]^{y}\in\mathcal{L}^{\infty}(\mu)
and
[\eta_{\bullet}(g)]^{y}\in\mathcal{L}^{\infty}(\mu)
do not both fail, and hence they both hold.
Thus,
\eta
generates
\mathcal{L}^{\infty}(\mu)
-sections and therefore is
\mu
-smooth.
∎

Theorem 8.25
.

Let
(X,\varSigma,\mu)
be complete and non-atomic and let
(Y,T,\nu)
be complete, non-atomic and perfect. If the pair
(\delta,\eta)\in\Lambda^{\infty}(\mu)\times\Lambda^{\infty}(\nu)
is
\mathcal{L}^{\infty}(\mu\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\nu)
-preserving and
\eta
is a
2
-marginal with respect to
\mathcal{L}^{\infty}(\mu\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\nu)
then
\delta\odot\eta\in{P}^{\infty}(\mu\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\nu)
but
\delta\odot\eta\notin\Lambda^{\infty}(\mu\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\nu)
.

Proof.

We have that
\eta
is a weak
2
-marginal with respect to
\mathcal{L}^{\infty}(\mu\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\nu)
by Remark
8.10
. That
\delta\odot\eta\in{P}^{\infty}(\mu\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\nu)
then follows from Proposition
8.19
.
For the last statement suppose we had
\delta\odot\eta\in\Lambda^{\infty}(\mu\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\nu)
.

By Proposition 9.24
\eta
is
\mu
-smooth, i.e., it is a 2-marginal with respect to
\mathcal{L}^{\infty}(\mu\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\nu)
and generates
\mathcal{L}^{\infty}(\mu)
-sections.
Since
\eta
is 2-marginal with respect to
\mathcal{L}^{\infty}(\mu\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\nu)
, the induced lifting on
T
, which we also denote by
\eta
, is a
2
-marginal with respect to
\varSigma\,\widehat{\otimes}\,{T}
. (The induced lifting on
T
is given by
B\mapsto\eta(B)
where
\eta(\chi_{B})=\chi_{\eta(B)}
. We have
\eta_{\bullet}(\chi_{E})=\chi_{\eta_{\bullet}(E)}
for any set
E\subseteq X\times Y
, as is readily verified.)
Thus, applying
[
3
]
Lemma 4.11 (see
[
5
]
Lemma 4.8 for an important correction to the proof
16
16

16

In
[
5
]
Lemma 4.8,
\xi\in\vartheta(\nu)
should read
\xi\in\vartheta(I_{2})
. In our application here, we have
\xi=\eta
.

) we obtain the existence of a lifting
\pi\in\Lambda(\mu\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\nu)
such that

(8.26)

\eta([\pi(E)]_{x})=[\pi(E)]_{x}\;\;\mbox{for all}\;\;E\in\varSigma\,\widehat{\otimes}\,{T}\;\;\mbox{and}\;\;x\in{X}.

By standard arguments it then follows that

\eta([\pi(f)]_{x})=[\pi(f)]_{x}
for all
f\in\mathcal{L}^{\infty}(\mu\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\nu)
and
x\in X
. (From (
8.26
) this holds for
f=\chi_{E}
,
E\in\varSigma\,\widehat{\otimes}\,{T}
, and hence for simple functions. Then use the fact that any
f\in\mathcal{L}^{\infty}(\mu\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\nu)
is a uniform limit of simple functions and the fact that liftings preserve uniform limits.)

Since
\eta
generates
\mathcal{L}^{\infty}(\mu)
-sections, and
[\eta_{\bullet}(f)]_{x}=\eta(f_{x})=\eta([\pi(f)]_{x})=[\pi(f)]_{x}
for
\mu
-almost all
x\in X
, it follows that
[\pi(f)]^{y}\in\mathcal{L}^{\infty}(\mu)
for all
f\in\mathcal{L}^{\infty}(\mu\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\nu)
and
y\in{Y}
. (Cf. the proof of Lemma 9.4.)
Thus, we have

(8.27)

[\pi(E)]^{y}\in\varSigma\;\;\mbox{for all}\;\;E\in\varSigma\,\widehat{\otimes}\,{T}\;\;\mbox{and}\;\;y\in{Y}.

So, there exist liftings
\eta\in\Lambda(\nu)
and
\pi\in\Lambda(\mu\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\nu)
satisfying conditions (
8.26
) and (
8.27
), which contradicts
[
25
]
Theorem 4.3.
∎
